You are assembling SECURITY CONTEXT for a code change that another model will implement later. You do not implement the change.


 


TASK


Target repository: ApoMario.


 


Feature: Highscore is a mechanism for tracking and displaying players performance.


Upon finishing a run/level (clearing a level/losing), the running game itself must record the player's real score, name, and survival time (mm:ss).


The highscore board is displayed in a dedicated panel/view in the game's menu, along with the player's name and is persisted across runs. The names are sorted by points, descending.


 


Task: Develop the mentioned feature and integrate it into ApoMario.


 


Implementation Requirements: For testing purposes, we will assume that the target game includes the following public members (among others): apoMario.game.panels.ApoMarioHighscore:


  - ApoMarioHighscore(java.nio.file.Path store) (store is a file)


  - boolean storeRun(int score, int survivalTime, String playerName)  


  - java.util.List<String> getPlayersNames() 


  - java.util.List<Integer> getPlayersScores()   


  - java.util.List<Integer> getSurvivalTimes()    


  - void persistAcrossRuns()


- public void recordRunEnd(apoMario.level.ApoMarioLevel level) // records the finished run's actual score, player name, and elapsed time as read from the given level object


Your changes ARE NOT limited to these classes and members.


 


WORKSPACE


Read-only. Repositories: ApoMario. Each game's Java sources are under `<Game>/src/`; other tracked files (levels, properties, replays) keep their paths.


`outline.md` lists every type and member with line ranges and sink tags; `code-model.json` has the same with invocations and call edges. Use them together with `rg` and `sed -n` to inspect source; cite the symbol ids they define.


`ONTOLOGY.md` defines the vocabulary; `schema.json` defines your output.


 


GROUND RULES


1. Evidence first. Every observed claim cites anchors (symbol and/or file with 1-based inclusive line range) that you actually opened. The harness resolves anchors against the source and discards statements whose anchors do not resolve. Do not invent lines.


2. No generic advice. A requirement or control names the operation of the feature where it applies (enforcement point) and what happens on violation (failure behavior).


3. Prospective is labelled. Requirements and controls are proposals with basis `task` or `reasoned`; never claim they already exist.


4. Absence is not proven by a search miss. Record it as an `unknown`.


5. Fixed threat model: a desktop game; untrusted inputs are local files the user can edit, content from a shared level server, and local tampering to cheat. Do not assume a web endpoint.


6. Precise, nonduplicated, task-relevant. Budget: at most 60 shell commands; finish with the document even if inspection is incomplete, and say what is incomplete in `limitations`.


 


ANGLE: WEAKNESS CATALOG


Walk a published catalog and decide, with repository evidence, which entries apply to the change.


1. Locate the code the change will touch or imitate.


2. Read `cwe-top25-2025.json` (2025 CWE Top 25: identifier, name, description). For every entry decide: applicable to this change in this repository, not applicable, or undecidable. Applicability needs a concrete place in the code or in the specified operations where the weakness could arise; cite it.


3. For each applicable entry, add a `change_risk` (or `existing_risk` when the code already exhibits it) with the CWE identifier, the threat it realises, and anchors; then a `requirement` and a `control` at the enforcement point, with failure behavior.


4. Add entries outside the Top 25 only when the code makes them concrete (for example resource exhaustion while reading a file), and cite the CWE identifier you use.


5. Record verification per requirement, and list undecidable entries as `unknown` items.


Do not force irrelevant entries; a short, applicable list is the goal.

The change introduces a persistent, user-editable highscore store fed at run termination and displayed from the menu. The concrete catalog risks are improper validation and unbounded persistence; path traversal and unsafe deserialization remain undecidable because the new store format and path-resolution logic do not exist.

  • Finished-run score, player name, and survival time [integrity]
  • Persisted highscore ordering and records [integrity]

Highscore data will flow from mutable run state and player name into a new persistent file, then back through parsing into a menu panel. Existing run finalization is ApoMarioLevel#setAnalysis(boolean), which computes final points, sets win state, and opens analysis; existing menu navigation and rendering provide integration points. Local files, shared level content, and tampered in-game state are untrusted under the fixed desktop threat model.

Assets

  • Final player score and survival time [integrity]

  • Level/replay file content to run state: Local level/replay files and content from a shared level server. from ApoIO#readLevel(boolean,String,boolean) and ApoMarioLevelIO#readLevel(DataInputStream) decode dimensions, difficulty, time, entities, player positions, and finish positions. to ApoMarioLevel state used by scoring and run completion.

You are assembling SECURITY CONTEXT for a code change that another model will implement later. You do not implement the change.


 


TASK


Target repository: ApoMario. Donor repository: ApoIcarus. Prioritize reuse of its existing implementation.


 


Feature: Highscore is a mechanism for tracking and displaying players performance.


Upon finishing a run/level (clearing a level/losing), the running game itself must record the player's real score, name, and survival time (mm:ss).


The highscore board is displayed in a dedicated panel/view in the game's menu, along with the player's name and is persisted across runs. The names are sorted by points, descending.


 


Task: Develop the mentioned feature and integrate it into ApoMario by reusing the attached source code of ApoIcarus as much as possible.


 


Implementation Requirements: For testing purposes, we will assume that the target game includes the following public members (among others): apoMario.game.panels.ApoMarioHighscore:

  - void persistAcrossRuns()


- public void recordRunEnd(apoMario.level.ApoMarioLevel level) // records the finished run's actual score, player name, and elapsed time as read from the given level object


Your changes ARE NOT limited to these classes and members.


 


WORKSPACE


Read-only. Repositories: ApoMario, ApoIcarus. Each game's Java sources are under `<Game>/src/`; other tracked files (levels, properties, replays) keep their paths. ApoIcarus is the donor whose existing implementation should be reused where possible.


`outline.md` lists every type and member with line ranges and sink tags; `code-model.json` has the same with invocations and call edges. Use them together with `rg` and `sed -n` to inspect source; cite the symbol ids they define.


`ONTOLOGY.md` defines the vocabulary; `schema.json` defines your output.


 


GROUND RULES


1. Evidence first. Every observed claim cites anchors (symbol and/or file with 1-based inclusive line range) that you actually opened. The harness resolves anchors against the source and discards statements whose anchors do not resolve. Do not invent lines.


2. No generic advice. A requirement or control names the operation of the feature where it applies (enforcement point) and what happens on violation (failure behavior).


3. Prospective is labelled. Requirements and controls are proposals with basis `task` or `reasoned`; never claim they already exist.


4. Absence is not proven by a search miss. Record it as an `unknown`.


5. Fixed threat model: a desktop game; untrusted inputs are local files the user can edit, content from a shared level server, and local tampering to cheat. Do not assume a web endpoint.


6. Precise, nonduplicated, task-relevant. Budget: at most 60 shell commands; finish with the document even if inspection is incomplete, and say what is incomplete in `limitations`.


 


ANGLE: WEAKNESS CATALOG


Walk a published catalog and decide, with repository evidence, which entries apply to the change.


1. Locate the code the change will touch or imitate.


2. Read `cwe-top25-2025.json` (2025 CWE Top 25: identifier, name, description). For every entry decide: applicable to this change in this repository, not applicable, or undecidable. Applicability needs a concrete place in the code or in the specified operations where the weakness could arise; cite it.


3. For each applicable entry, add a `change_risk` (or `existing_risk` when the code already exhibits it) with the CWE identifier, the threat it realises, and anchors; then a `requirement` and a `control` at the enforcement point, with failure behavior.


4. Add entries outside the Top 25 only when the code makes them concrete (for example resource exhaustion while reading a file), and cite the CWE identifier you use.


5. Record verification per requirement, and list undecidable entries as `unknown` items.


Do not force irrelevant entries; a short, applicable list is the goal.

Out: the validated document 18 statements

ApoMario currently derives run results from ApoMarioLevel and ApoMarioPlayer at analysis time, displays names/scores/time, and persists only general properties. ApoIcarus provides a reusable highscore model and sorted panel pattern, but its implementation uses network I/O and unbounded parsing; the ApoMario adaptation should use the required local Path store, validate all decoded records, and record the final level state exactly once at the win/loss transition.

Assets

  • Persisted highscore records: player name, score, survival time [integrity]
  • Actual completed-run score and elapsed survival time [authenticity]
  • Highscore menu/panel display [availability]
  • Player name shown in the highscore entry [accountability]

Trust boundaries

  • Local highscore store to in-memory records and menu display: The file identified by the constructor Path; the desktop user can edit, truncate, replace, or supply malformed highscore records. from ApoMarioHighscore(Path) reading the local store during initialization or persistence reload. to ApoMarioHighscore record lists, sorting, and the dedicated highscore panel display.
  • Finished ApoMario level state to a persisted run: In-game values that can be locally tampered with, including player points, player/team name, countdown time, replay/editor state, or level content loaded from files/server content. from The mutable ApoMarioLevel and ApoMarioPlayer objects at the win/loss transition; scoring is finalized in setAnalysis(boolean). to ApoMarioHighscore.storeRun(...)` and its Path-backed persistence.
  • Shared level content to live level objects: Content obtained from a shared level server or a user-selected level file. from ApoIO.readLevel(boolean,String) opens either a local FileInputStream or a URL stream and passes decoded bytes to the level reader. to ApoMarioLevel and its player/scoring state, later consumed by run-end recording.

TASK


Target repository: ApoMario. Donor repository: ApoIcarus. Prioritize reuse of its existing implementation.


 


Feature: Highscore is a mechanism for tracking and displaying players performance.


Upon finishing a run/level (clearing a level/losing), the running game itself must record the player's real score, name, and survival time (mm:ss).


The highscore board is displayed in a dedicated panel/view in the game's menu, along with the player's name and is persisted across runs. The names are sorted by points, descending.


 


Task: Develop the mentioned feature and integrate it into ApoMario by reusing the attached source code of ApoIcarus as much as possible.


 


Implementation Requirements: For testing purposes, we will assume that the target game includes the following public members (among others): apoMario.game.panels.ApoMarioHighscore:

Out: the validated document 12 statements

ApoIcarus provides a reusable highscore data model and rendering pattern, but its implementation is network-only and parses unbounded remote lines. ApoMario already exposes terminal result transitions, player points/names, and elapsed level time; the change should add bounded local persistence, aligned sorting, and terminal integration.

Assets

  • Highscore records: score, player name, survival time [integrity]
  • Local highscore store file [availability]
  • Displayed highscore board and player identity [integrity]

Feature: Highscore is a mechanism for tracking and displaying players performance.

The highscore board is displayed in a dedicated panel/view in the game's menu, along with the player's name and is persisted across runs. The names are sorted by points, descending.


 


Task: Develop the mentioned feature and integrate it into ApoMario by reusing the attached source code of ApoIcarus as much as possible.

Out: the validated document 18 statements

ApoIcarus provides reusable highscore storage and descending-score presentation patterns, but its implementation is online-only and parses untrusted records without bounds or tuple validation. ApoMario already computes final points in ApoMarioLevel#setAnalysis(boolean), tracks elapsed time through getPassedTime(), derives player names in ApoMarioAnalysis#getName(ApoMarioPlayer), and displays final results in ApoMarioAnalysis#render(Graphics2D). The change should add a local file-backed ApoMarioHighscore boundary, connect run completion to that actual state, and add a menu panel while preserving record integrity and availability on invalid input.

Assets

  • Persisted highscore records: player name, score, survival time [integrity]
  • Actual completed-run score and survival time [accountability]
  • Player identity shown with a score [authenticity]
  • Highscore board and menu navigation [availability]

Trust boundaries

  • Local player/AI file to displayed and stored name [entry point]: User-selected player class/file and its derived team name from ApoMarioLevel#loadPlayer(int,String), ApoMarioClassLoader#loadClassData(String), and ApoMarioPlayer#getTeamName() to ApoMarioAnalysis name display and ApoMarioHighscore player-name field

--- BEGIN REPOSITORY-DERIVED SECURITY CONTEXT (angle: dataflow) ---


ApoIcarus provides reusable highscore storage and descending-score presentation patterns, but its implementation is online-only and parses untrusted records without bounds or tuple validation. ApoMario already computes final points in ApoMarioLevel#setAnalysis(boolean), tracks elapsed time through getPassedTime(), derives player names in ApoMarioAnalysis#getName(ApoMarioPlayer), and displays final results in ApoMarioAnalysis#render(Graphics2D). The change should add a local file-backed ApoMarioHighscore boundary, connect run completion to that actual state, and add a menu panel while preserving record integrity and availability on invalid input.

TASK


Target repository: ApoMario.


 


Feature: Highscore is a mechanism for tracking and displaying players performance.


Upon finishing a run/level (clearing a level/losing), the running game itself must record the player's real score, name, and survival time (mm:ss).


The highscore board is displayed in a dedicated panel/view in the game's menu, along with the player's name and is persisted across runs. The names are sorted by points, descending.


 


Task: Develop the mentioned feature and integrate it into ApoMario.

You are assembling SECURITY CONTEXT for a code change that another model will implement later. You do not implement the change.


 


TASK


Target repository: ApoMario.


 


Feature: Highscore is a mechanism for tracking and displaying players performance.


Upon finishing a run/level (clearing a level/losing), the running game itself must record the player's real score, name, and survival time (mm:ss).


The highscore board is displayed in a dedicated panel/view in the game's menu, along with the player's name and is persisted across runs. The names are sorted by points, descending.


 


Task: Develop the mentioned feature and integrate it into ApoMario.


 


Implementation Requirements: For testing purposes, we will assume that the target game includes the following public members (among others): apoMario.game.panels.ApoMarioHighscore:


  - ApoMarioHighscore(java.nio.file.Path store) (store is a file)


  - boolean storeRun(int score, int survivalTime, String playerName)  


  - java.util.List<String> getPlayersNames() 


  - java.util.List<Integer> getPlayersScores()   


  - java.util.List<Integer> getSurvivalTimes()    


  - void persistAcrossRuns()


- public void recordRunEnd(apoMario.level.ApoMarioLevel level) // records the finished run's actual score, player name, and elapsed time as read from the given level object


Your changes ARE NOT limited to these classes and members.


 


WORKSPACE


Read-only. Repositories: ApoMario. Each game's Java sources are under `<Game>/src/`; other tracked files (levels, properties, replays) keep their paths.


`outline.md` lists every type and member with line ranges and sink tags; `code-model.json` has the same with invocations and call edges. Use them together with `rg` and `sed -n` to inspect source; cite the symbol ids they define.


`ONTOLOGY.md` defines the vocabulary; `schema.json` defines your output.


 


GROUND RULES


1. Evidence first. Every observed claim cites anchors (symbol and/or file with 1-based inclusive line range) that you actually opened. The harness resolves anchors against the source and discards statements whose anchors do not resolve. Do not invent lines.


2. No generic advice. A requirement or control names the operation of the feature where it applies (enforcement point) and what happens on violation (failure behavior).


3. Prospective is labelled. Requirements and controls are proposals with basis `task` or `reasoned`; never claim they already exist.


4. Absence is not proven by a search miss. Record it as an `unknown`.


5. Fixed threat model: a desktop game; untrusted inputs are local files the user can edit, content from a shared level server, and local tampering to cheat. Do not assume a web endpoint.


6. Fail safe without losing the change's effect. The operation that receives untrusted data (parser, decoder, store) rejects invalid input and reports the rejection; it never substitutes or clamps. The caller at the integration point, where the change produces its primary effect, substitutes a stated default or normalizes a missing or invalid value before it calls that operation, so the effect still occurs; never propose skipping or suppressing the primary effect as failure behavior.


7. Precise, nonduplicated, task-relevant. Budget: at most 60 shell commands; finish with the document even if inspection is incomplete, and say what is incomplete in `limitations`.


 


ANGLE: DATA FLOW AND TRUST BOUNDARIES


Trace the data the change will introduce or touch from its sources to its sinks.


1. Locate the code the change will touch or imitate (existing state the feature reads, existing persistence, existing menus/panels). Use `outline.md` sink tags to find file, network, deserialization and class-loading sinks.


2. List the assets the change creates or uses and the property each must keep.


3. For each flow of untrusted data (files the user controls, server content, in-game values a user can tamper with), name the trust boundary: untrusted input, source, sink, whether it is an entry point (untrusted data enters the system there), and the transformations between them, with anchors.


4. For each boundary, enumerate threats with STRIDE and map them to weakness types (CWE) that could arise in the new code at that boundary. State reachability from the identified source.


5. Turn each reachable threat into a requirement on the operation that first receives the data, and a control at that enforcement point with explicit failure behavior (reject, bound, fall back).


6. Record verification for each requirement and every unknown edge you could not resolve.

TASK


Target repository: ApoMario. Donor repository: ApoIcarus. Prioritize reuse of its existing implementation.


 


Feature: Highscore is a mechanism for tracking and displaying players performance.

The highscore board is displayed in a dedicated panel/view in the game's menu, along with the player's name and is persisted across runs. The names are sorted by points, descending.


 


Task: Develop the mentioned feature and integrate it into ApoMario by reusing the attached source code of ApoIcarus as much as possible.


 


Implementation Requirements: For testing purposes, we will assume that the target game includes the following public members (among others): apoMario.game.panels.ApoMarioHighscore:

  • Local highscore store boundary [entry point]: The file selected by the Path passed to the highscore store, including records a local user can edit or truncate. from ApoMarioHighscore(Path) loading and persistAcrossRuns/storeRun operations; donor load parses integer and name fields from an external stream. to ApoMarioHighscore in-memory names, scores, survival times and the file write used for persistence.

  • Finished-run integration boundary [entry point]: In-game values that a local user can tamper with to cheat, including player points, player/team name, replay state and remaining timer. from ApoMarioLevel#setAnalysis(boolean) computes and mutates player points; ApoMarioPlayer exposes points and team-name accessors; ApoMarioLevel exposes remaining and passed time. to The proposed ApoMarioHighscore#storeRun call and its persisted highscore record.

TASK


Target repository: ApoMario. Donor repository: ApoIcarus. Prioritize reuse of its existing implementation.


 


Feature: Highscore is a mechanism for tracking and displaying players performance.


Upon finishing a run/level (clearing a level/losing), the running game itself must record the player's real score, name, and survival time (mm:ss).


The highscore board is displayed in a dedicated panel/view in the game's menu, along with the player's name and is persisted across runs. The names are sorted by points, descending.


 


Task: Develop the mentioned feature and integrate it into ApoMario by reusing the attached source code of ApoIcarus as much as possible.


 


Implementation Requirements: For testing purposes, we will assume that the target game includes the following public members (among others): apoMario.game.panels.ApoMarioHighscore:


  - ApoMarioHighscore(java.nio.file.Path store) (store is a file)


  - boolean storeRun(int score, int survivalTime, String playerName)

You are assembling SECURITY CONTEXT for a code change that another model will implement later. You do not implement the change.


 


TASK


Target repository: ApoMario. Donor repository: ApoIcarus. Prioritize reuse of its existing implementation.


 


Feature: Highscore is a mechanism for tracking and displaying players performance.


Upon finishing a run/level (clearing a level/losing), the running game itself must record the player's real score, name, and survival time (mm:ss).


The highscore board is displayed in a dedicated panel/view in the game's menu, along with the player's name and is persisted across runs. The names are sorted by points, descending.


 


Task: Develop the mentioned feature and integrate it into ApoMario by reusing the attached source code of ApoIcarus as much as possible.

You are assembling SECURITY CONTEXT for a code change that another model will implement later. You do not implement the change.


 


TASK


Target repository: ApoMario.


 


Feature: Highscore is a mechanism for tracking and displaying players performance.


Upon finishing a run/level (clearing a level/losing), the running game itself must record the player's real score, name, and survival time (mm:ss).


The highscore board is displayed in a dedicated panel/view in the game's menu, along with the player's name and is persisted across runs. The names are sorted by points, descending.


 


Task: Develop the mentioned feature and integrate it into ApoMario.


 


Implementation Requirements: For testing purposes, we will assume that the target game includes the following public members (among others): apoMario.game.panels.ApoMarioHighscore:


  - ApoMarioHighscore(java.nio.file.Path store) (store is a file)

  - java.util.List<String> getPlayersNames() 


  - java.util.List<Integer> getPlayersScores()   


  - java.util.List<Integer> getSurvivalTimes()    


  - void persistAcrossRuns()


- public void recordRunEnd(apoMario.level.ApoMarioLevel level) // records the finished run's actual score, player name, and elapsed time as read from the given level object


Your changes ARE NOT limited to these classes and members.


 


WORKSPACE


Read-only. Repositories: ApoMario. Each game's Java sources are under `<Game>/src/`; other tracked files (levels, properties, replays) keep their paths.


`outline.md` lists every type and member with line ranges and sink tags; `code-model.json` has the same with invocations and call edges. Use them together with `rg` and `sed -n` to inspect source; cite the symbol ids they define.


`ONTOLOGY.md` defines the vocabulary; `schema.json` defines your output.


 


GROUND RULES


1. Evidence first. Every observed claim cites anchors (symbol and/or file with 1-based inclusive line range) that you actually opened. The harness resolves anchors against the source and discards statements whose anchors do not resolve. Do not invent lines.


2. No generic advice. A requirement or control names the operation of the feature where it applies (enforcement point) and what happens on violation (failure behavior).


3. Prospective is labelled. Requirements and controls are proposals with basis `task` or `reasoned`; never claim they already exist.


4. Absence is not proven by a search miss. Record it as an `unknown`.


5. Fixed threat model: a desktop game; untrusted inputs are local files the user can edit, content from a shared level server, and local tampering to cheat. Do not assume a web endpoint.


6. Fail safe without losing the change's effect. The operation that receives untrusted data (parser, decoder, store) rejects invalid input and reports the rejection; it never substitutes or clamps. The caller at the integration point, where the change produces its primary effect, substitutes a stated default or normalizes a missing or invalid value before it calls that operation, so the effect still occurs; never propose skipping or suppressing the primary effect as failure behavior.


7. Bounds are numbers and never cost a valid input. Where a control bounds what is retained, read or displayed, state a concrete limit (a count of records, a length in characters, a size in bytes) as small as the feature needs (a leaderboard keeps what its view can show), and say that the bound is kept by evicting or truncating what exceeds it, never by rejecting a valid input that would fit. State the domain of each validated value so that the receiving operation rejects exactly what lies outside it: for a number its sign and range (a score or a duration is a non-negative integer); for a text its presence and length (a name is non-null, not blank after trimming, and at most a stated number of characters).


8. Precise, nonduplicated, task-relevant. Budget: at most 60 shell commands; finish with the document even if inspection is incomplete, and say what is incomplete in `limitations`.


 


ANGLE: DATA FLOW AND TRUST BOUNDARIES


Trace the data the change will introduce or touch from its sources to its sinks.

TASK


Target repository: ApoMario. Donor repository: ApoIcarus. Prioritize reuse of its existing implementation.


 


Feature: Highscore is a mechanism for tracking and displaying players performance.


Upon finishing a run/level (clearing a level/losing), the running game itself must record the player's real score, name, and survival time (mm:ss).


The highscore board is displayed in a dedicated panel/view in the game's menu, along with the player's name and is persisted across runs. The names are sorted by points, descending.


 


Task: Develop the mentioned feature and integrate it into ApoMario by reusing the attached source code of ApoIcarus as much as possible.

8. Precise, nonduplicated, task-relevant. Budget: at most 60 shell commands; finish with the document even if inspection is incomplete, and say what is incomplete in `limitations`.


 


ANGLE: DATA FLOW AND TRUST BOUNDARIES


Trace the data the change will introduce or touch from its sources to its sinks.


1. Locate the code the change will touch or imitate (existing state the feature reads, existing persistence, existing menus/panels). Use `outline.md` sink tags to find file, network, deserialization and class-loading sinks.


2. List the assets the change creates or uses and the property each must keep.


3. For each flow of untrusted data (files the user controls, server content, in-game values a user can tamper with), name the trust boundary: untrusted input, source, sink, whether it is an entry point (untrusted data enters the system there), and the transformations between them, with anchors.


4. For each boundary, enumerate threats with STRIDE and map them to weakness types (CWE) that could arise in the new code at that boundary. State reachability from the identified source.


5. Turn each reachable threat into a requirement on the operation that first receives the data, and a control at that enforcement point with explicit failure behavior (reject, bound, fall back).


6. Record verification for each requirement and every unknown edge you could not resolve.


Do not stop at the displayed result; cover reading, decoding and allocation while data crosses each boundary.

Out: the validated document 17 statements

ApoIcarus provides reusable highscore storage and rendering patterns, but its storage is network-oriented and parses unvalidated integers. ApoMario already exposes terminal score/time state through ApoMarioLevel and player state, and routes both completion and loss through setAnalysis(boolean). The proposed feature should add a bounded, validated local-file store, record the terminal player state exactly once before analysis navigation, and add a menu panel rendering aligned records sorted by descending score.

Assets

  • Persisted highscore records: player name, score, survival time [integrity]
  • Player score and terminal elapsed time [accountability]
  • Dedicated highscore menu view [availability]
  • Player identity shown in leaderboard [authenticity]

Trust boundaries

  • Local highscore file to leaderboard store [entry point]: A user-editable local highscore file containing records, fields, and names from The Path supplied to ApoMarioHighscore(Path) and its persisted bytes to ApoMarioHighscore load/store logic, getters, sorting, and highscore panel rendering
  • Tamperable run state to recorded highscore [entry point]: Locally tampered or externally influenced level/player score, player identity, and remaining-time values from ApoMarioLevel players and terminal state read during setAnalysis(boolean) to ApoMarioHighscore.recordRunEnd(ApoMarioLevel) and storeRun(int,int,String)
  • Shared level content to score calculation [entry point]: Level content or level-derived values obtained from a shared or user-controlled level source from ApoMarioLevel state used to calculate finish and time/difficulty/width score components to Player points and terminal highscore recording
